Oh, hello Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me today. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your busy schedule. 

Well... I came in because the ladies at my church were a little concerned about me. It's really nothing terrible, I promise, just... I guess I've been doing some slightly silly things lately. I help out at the potluck table, you see. But recently, I... well, sometimes I'll just stand there holding the serving spoon up in the air, and I just sort of... stop moving for a bit. Is... is that the sort of thing you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you didn’t feel much interest or pleasure in activities you usually enjoy, like helping at the potluck table?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I still love it very much. Helping out at the church is... well, it's just my favorite part of the week. I wouldn't want anyone to think I don't care about it anymore, or that I'm being ungrateful. \n\nI always look forward to seeing everyone and doing my part. Even when my mind wanders and I get a little stuck, like I was saying, I'm still so happy to be there. I really do enjoy it, I promise. I hope that's okay to say? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or sad lately, even when you're at church or doing things you enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor. I really haven't been feeling sad or down at all. I mean, everyone has a gloomy afternoon now and then, I suppose, but nothing out of the ordinary. \n\nI try to keep a very cheerful disposition. I know nobody likes a sourpuss, especially not at church! I just count my blessings and try to keep a smile on my face. I wouldn't want to bring anyone else's spirits down, you know? \n\nI hope I'm being helpful. I'm trying my best to answer everything just how you need me to. Is there... is there anything else I can clarify for you about that?\n\nDoctor: Can you describe exactly what goes through your mind when you're holding the serving spoon or folding the napkins repeatedly? No, I still love it very much. Helping out at the church is... well, it's just my favorite part of the week. I wouldn't want anyone to think I don't care about it anymore, or that I'm being ungrateful. \n\nI always look forward to seeing everyone and doing my part. Even when my mind wanders and I get a little stuck, like I was saying, I'm still so happy to be there. I really do enjoy it, I promise. I hope that's okay to say? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or sad lately, even when you're at church or doing things you enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor. I really haven't been feeling sad or down at all. I mean, everyone has a gloomy afternoon now and then, I suppose, but nothing out of the ordinary. \n\nI try to keep a very cheerful disposition. I know nobody likes a sourpuss, especially not at church! I just count my blessings and try to keep a smile on my face. I wouldn't want to bring anyone else's spirits down, you know? \n\nI hope I'm being helpful. I'm trying my best to answer everything just how you need me to. Is there... is there anything else I can clarify for you about that?\n\nDoctor: Can you describe exactly what goes through your mind when you're holding the serving spoon or folding the napkins repeatedly? Do you feel like you're in a daze or that you're not fully aware of what you're doing during those moments?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... that’s a very good question, Doctor. I... I’ve actually tried to figure that out myself, to be honest. But... really, nothing much at all goes through my mind. It’s just... quiet, I suppose. \n\nI wouldn't say I'm in a daze, exactly. Oh, goodness, 'daze' makes it sound like I'm ignoring people or not paying attention, and I promise I always try my very best to be attentive. I'd hate for anyone to think I was being rude! It's more like... I just sort of lose my place for a second. Like I'm taking a little pause, and I just don't quite realize I've stopped moving until someone says my name or taps my shoulder. \n\nI'm fully awake, I promise! I just... I don't really have a good excuse for why I do it. I hope that doesn't make me sound foolish, or like I'm not trying hard enough to do my job at the table. Does that make sense, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm explaining it right for your notes.\n\nDoctor: During those moments when you're holding the spoon or folding the napkins and you pause, do you feel like you're completely aware of everything around you, or does the world seem a little different, distant, or unreal?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, the world doesn't seem unreal at all. Everything is just as it should be. I wouldn't want you to think I'm losing touch with things or anything like that. I know exactly where I am—right there in the church hall with everyone else.\n\nIt doesn't feel distant or strange, I promise. I can still hear the chatter and the clinking of the plates, it's just... I suppose I'm just very focused on the spoon, or the napkin, for that little moment. It's almost like I'm just being very, very careful with what I'm holding, even if I don't need to be. \n\nI really am right there with everyone, though. I don't really have a good excuse for why I do it. I hope that doesn't make me sound foolish, or like I'm not trying hard enough to do my job at the table. Does that make sense, Doctor? Everything is just as it should be. I wouldn't want you to think I'm losing touch with things or anything like that. I know exactly where I am—right there in the church hall with everyone else.\n\nIt doesn't feel distant or strange, I promise. I can still hear the chatter and the clinking of the plates, it's just... I suppose I'm just very focused on the spoon, or the napkin, for that little moment. It's almost like I'm just being very, very careful with what I'm holding, even if I don't need to be. \n\nI really am right there with everyone, though. I'd never want to seem like I'm ignoring the lovely people at church or off in my own little world. That would be so impolite! I hope that's the right kind of answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture of how it is.\n\nDoctor: When you come out of those paused moments, do you ever feel confused about what just happened, or have trouble remembering what you were doing before you paused?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I don't feel confused at all. My memory is still quite sharp, thank the Lord. I wouldn't want you to think I'm getting... well, senile or anything like that! \n\nWhen someone says my name and I realize I've stopped, I just... I go right back to what I was doing. I know exactly that I was serving the green beans or smoothing out the linens. I just pick right up where I left off, usually with an apology and a smile. I try to be very quick about it so nobody is kept waiting, because I'd just hate to be an inconvenience to anyone at the luncheon. \n\nI hope... I hope that's a helpful answer? I've never had anything like that happen. No twitches or jerking about. I've always been very steady, thank the Lord. I try to be very composed, you know, especially around the other ladies. \n\nMy hands and legs stay right where I put them. Oh, goodness, 'daze' makes it sound like I'm ignoring people or not paying attention, and I promise I always try my very best to be attentive. I'd hate for anyone to think I was being rude! It's more like... I just sort of lose my place for a second. Like I'm taking a little pause, and I just don't quite realize I've stopped moving until someone says my name or taps my shoulder. \n\nI'm fully awake, I promise! I just... I don't really have a good excuse for why I do it. I hope that doesn't make me sound foolish, or like I'm not trying hard enough to do my job at the table. Does that make sense, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm explaining it right for your notes.\n\nDoctor: During those moments when you're holding the spoon or folding the napkins and you pause, do you feel like you're completely aware of everything around you, or does the world seem a little different, distant, or unreal?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, the world doesn't seem unreal at all. Everything is just as it should be. I wouldn't want you to think I'm losing touch with things or anything like that. I know exactly where I am—right there in the church hall with everyone else.\n\nIt doesn't feel distant or strange, I promise. I can still hear the chatter and the clinking of the plates, it's just... I suppose I'm just very focused on the spoon, or the napkin, for that little moment. It's almost like I'm just being very, very careful with what I'm holding, even if I don't need to be. \n\nI really am right there with everyone, though. I'd never want to seem like I'm ignoring the lovely people at church or off in my own little world. That would be so impolite! I hope that's the right kind of answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture of how it is.\n\nDoctor: When you come out of those paused moments, do you ever feel confused about what just happened, or have trouble remembering what you were doing before you paused?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I don't feel confused at all. My memory is still quite sharp, thank the Lord. I wouldn't want you to think I'm getting... well, senile or anything like that! \n\nWhen someone says my name and I realize I've stopped, I just... I go right back to what I was doing. I know exactly that I was serving the green beans or smoothing out the linens. I just pick right up where I left off, usually with an apology and a smile. I try to be very quick about it so nobody is kept waiting, because I'd just hate to be an inconvenience to anyone at the luncheon. \n\nI hope... I hope that's a helpful answer? Am I explaining things alright for you, Doctor? I just want to be sure I'm doing a good job for your evaluation.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself making repeated movements, like lip smacking, picking at your clothes, or other actions you don’t remember doing while you were doing them?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens, no. No, I don't do anything like that. Lip smacking? Oh my goodness, I certainly hope not! That wouldn't be very good company manners, would it? I always try to be very proper when I'm out and about with folks. \n\nAnd I definitely don't pick at my clothes, either. I make sure to keep myself neat and tidy, and I just keep my hands folded in my lap if I'm not doing anything. I wouldn't want anyone thinking I looked unkempt or fidgety. \n\nI really do remember everything I do, Doctor. My hands don't just... go off on their own without me knowing. I'm always in control of myself, I promise. Does that... does that answer your question alright? I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need for your paperwork.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any sudden, brief, and involuntary movements, like a twitch or jerk in your arm, leg, or face, that you couldn’t control and weren’t doing on purpose?\n\nPatient: Oh, my, no. I've never had anything like that happen. No twitches or jerking about. I've always been very steady, thank the Lord. I wouldn't want to be suddenly jerking my arms or making faces... oh, that would just be so embarrassing! I try to be very composed, you know, especially around the other ladies. \n\nMy hands and legs stay right where I put them. Well, sometimes my knees get a little stiff just from my age, but they certainly don't jump around on their own or anything like that. \n\nI hope... I hope that's what you were asking about? I'm trying my very best to give you all the right information. Am I doing alright with these questions, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Before you find yourself pausing or getting stuck while holding the spoon or folding napkins, do you ever feel any unusual sensations, like a strange feeling in your stomach, a tingling, or something else that feels odd right before it happens?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor. I don't feel anything strange like that at all. My stomach is usually just fine... well, unless I've had a bit too much of the church coffee, but certainly no tingling or odd feelings before I... before I pause. \n\nIt just sort of happens without any warning, I suppose. I feel perfectly fine right up until I stop, and perfectly fine right after. I hope... is that a good sign, Doctor? I'd hate to think I was missing something I was supposed to be noticing, or that there was something wrong with my stomach on top of everything else. \n\nI'm trying to pay very close attention to myself so I can give you the right answers. Am I still doing okay for your notes?\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you're in the middle of those pauses, do you ever feel like you're doing something automatically, without being fully aware of it, like your body is moving on its own?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens no. No, my body definitely doesn't move on its own. When I have those little pauses, I'm just... well, I'm just sitting very still, that's all. I'm not doing anything automatic or moving about without knowing it. \n\nI'd be terribly upset if I thought I was doing things and not even realizing it! I always try to be very present and mindful of what I'm doing, especially if I'm helping out with the napkins or something useful. I just... I just stop for a moment, and then I'm right back to myself. \n\nIs it... is it better that I'm just staying still, Doctor? I'm hoping that means it's nothing too serious. I just want to make sure I'm being a good patient and giving you the answers you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself doing things like lip smacking, chewing, or making any other small, repeated movements without realizing you were doing them, especially during those paused moments?\n\nPatient: Oh, my goodness, no. I certainly hope not! I would be so embarrassed if I was sitting there smacking my lips or chewing on nothing in front of everyone. No, I've never noticed anything like that, and... well, surely one of the other ladies would have mentioned it to me if I was making faces or doing strange things with my mouth. \n\nWhen I have those pauses, I'm just very quiet and still. I wouldn't want to be making a spectacle of myself, you know? I try to keep very tidy and proper. \n\nI'm... I'm not supposed to be doing those things, am I? I hope it's a good thing that I'm just sitting quietly. I want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information, Doctor. Am I still answering everything the way you need me to?\n\nDoctor: During those pauses, are there ever moments where you feel confused, drowsy, or less alert—like you're not fully awake or aware of what's happening around you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, not at all. I don't feel confused or drowsy... I'm very much awake. I just... I just pause for a little bit, like I said. But I'm not sleepy or anything like that. \n\nI always try to stay very sharp, especially when I'm helping out. I wouldn't want anyone to think I was dozing off in the middle of the day! That wouldn't look very good at all, would it? \n\nI just stop for a moment, and then I go right back to what I was doing. I hope that's the right kind of answer? I'm really trying to be helpful and tell you exactly how it is. It's... it's a good thing that I'm not getting confused, right?\n\nDoctor: Before one of these pauses happens, do you ever notice any warning signs, like a strange smell, a sound, a visual change, or a funny feeling in your body that comes right before it starts?\n\nPatient: Oh, my. No, nothing like that. No strange smells or... or seeing things change. That sounds a bit frightening, to be honest! I hope I'm not supposed to be feeling those warning things? I would certainly tell you if I did, I promise. I don't want to leave anything out, I just want to be as helpful as I can. \n\nIt's a good sign that I'm not smelling strange things or getting funny feelings, isn't it, Doctor? I'm hoping that means I'm doing quite well.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had someone tell you that you looked dazed, unresponsive, or confused, even if you don't remember feeling that way yourself?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... no. No one has ever said anything like that to me. Dazed or confused? Goodness, I certainly hope I don't look like that! \n\nThe other ladies, they just... well, they just wait a moment for me to catch up if I pause. They're very polite about it. I just want to be sure I'm doing a good job for your evaluation.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself making repeated movements, like lip smacking, picking at your clothes, or other actions you don’t remember doing while you were doing them?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens, no. No, I don't do anything like that. Lip smacking? Oh my goodness, I certainly hope not! That wouldn't be very good company manners, would it? I make sure to keep myself neat and tidy, and I just keep my hands folded in my lap if I'm not doing anything. I wouldn't want anyone thinking I looked unkempt or fidgety. \n\nI really do remember everything I do, Doctor. My hands don't just... go off on their own without me knowing. I'm always in control of myself, I promise. Does that... does that answer your question alright?
